## Document 022: 20261005_投資週報_製造業加速擴張，AI需求推動市場

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261005_投資週報_製造業加速擴張，AI需求推動市場.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 05 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國製造業維持擴張，成本壓力升高：9 月 ISM 製造業調查顯示，美國製造業連續第九個月
位於擴張區間，新訂單與就業分項均見改善，反映接單與用人需求仍有支撐。然而，價格指數
由 8 月的 71.1 升至 9 月的 77.9，能源、金屬及關稅相關成本上升，加上供應商交貨偏慢，企
業面臨更大成本壓力 。 後續須觀察企業能否轉嫁成本 ， 以及通膨黏性對聯準會政策判斷的影響 。
◼ 日本短觀顯示製造業改善、服務業略緩：日本銀行 10 月 1 日公布的 9 月短觀顯示，大型製造

業景氣判斷指數由 6 月的＋22 升至＋24，大型非製造業則由＋37 降至＋35。企業預估 12 月
兩項指數分別回落至＋21 與＋30，顯示現況仍有支撐，後續看法則偏審慎。製造業改善有助
支撐日股獲利預期，但服務業放緩及日銀政策路徑仍須持續觀察。
◼ 台灣製造業活動加速擴張，AI 需求續強：10 月 1 日公布的製造業調查顯示，台灣 9 月製造業
活動擴張速度創 2021 年 8 月以來最快，AI 相關應用需求仍是重要推力。國發會 9 月 29 日公
布的 8 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與 7 月持平，領先及同時指標皆續
升。科技供應鏈景氣仍有支撐，但成長動能能否擴及其他產業，仍是觀察後續景氣的重點。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，關注財報季表現： AI 基礎建設需求未受利率環境影響，而是持續擴張，帶
動光通訊、CCL、ABF 載板 、記憶體、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持強勁成長，市場表
現由全面上漲轉向結構性分化。隨新一代 AI 伺服器放量、CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優

勢、定價能力及訂單能見度延伸至 2027 年的龍頭廠商仍可望受惠。10 月中旬起陸續公布的
第三季財報，若結果持續表現亮眼，可望為市場再添動能，屆時具 AI 供應鏈題材的台股與日
股亦可望受惠。
◼ 債券佈局聚焦 5~10 年期美國公債及投等債：雖然貨幣政策風險 下降有助於穩定長期通膨預
期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長
天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，5~10 年期債券較能兼顧票息收益與存續
期間風險管理。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公司債指數利差約 80-90 個基點，雖然利差
偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供具吸引力的絕對收益水準，因此現階段5~10 年期美國公債及
投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.50-159.00

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1400 
前週交易區間 
1.1214 1.1391

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.29-115.10.02 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週為提存期底 ， 繼央行上週大砍存單效應持續發酵 ， 市
場資金供給仍充裕，惟需求疲弱，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投仍冷清，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三於處所成交在 1.82%，10 年
期指標券則未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價為 1.95%。 
第四季台債發行量雖偏少 ， 惟美伊和談依舊陷入僵局 ， 國際油價續處高位 ， 通
膨疑慮尚存，國際債市走勢仍偏空，預期台債殖利率短線將維持高檔盤整格
局，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.95%~2%。

1 
2408 南亞科(Nanya Technology)

報告日期： 2026/10/13 研 究 員：翁浩軒  (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 毛利率優於預期且將維持 8 成以上 
 2027 年 DRAM 供需仍緊俏 
 新廠 2027 年第四季加入貢獻

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 25.4 元 
南亞科 3Q26 營收 1336.49 億(QoQ+61.9% 、YoY+611.7%) ，其中位元
出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合
約重新簽訂之調整)，匯率影響有利 1~3%，3Q26 毛利率上升至
85.7%，主要因平均銷售單價上升，營業費用季增 28 億元，因當
期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

二. 4Q26 整體產業仍將維持供不應求態勢  
受惠於 AI 需求 ， AI 運算（GPU 、 TPU 、 CPU 及 ASIC）成為 DRAM 
需求成長主力，帶動高容量、高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求，
範疇從 HBM 、LPDDR5 進一步擴展至網路、儲存與系統管理等領
域。在傳統應用市場方面，由於 DRAM 供給不足與 ASP 上漲，
部分傳統應用終端出現產品降規或延長生命週期的現象，低階市
場則有回收 DRAM 支撐部分需求。供給端方面，因新廠建置、設
備交期、製程導入均需要較長的前置時間，目前供給仍維持緊俏，
智慧型手機、個人電腦及一般消費性記憶體供給受限，製造商與
供應鏈端的庫存水位普遍偏低 ， 隨著供應商依 LTA 長約進行產品
組合與產能規劃，有利未來供需穩定度。

三. 新廠預估 4Q27 開始投產 
公司 5A 新廠預計 2027 年第一季裝機，2027 年第四季開始投
產 ， 預計 2028 年底前達到每月投片量 3 萬片 ， 全產能目標於 2030 
年底前達每月 5 萬片，未來產能擴充均以客戶長期合約的 AI 專
案為主要目標。另外公司亦佈局先進封測領域，規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠 ， 預估 2H28 設備進駐 。 公司 2026
年前三季累計資本支出為 276 億元 ， 2026 年資本支出以不超過 697
億元為上限，其中生產設備資本支出約占五成。

四.  2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 77.1 元/ 134.3 元 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：670 元 
       5 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：527.0 元 
     3.92 X 2027 EPS

展望 2026 下半年至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大 ， 接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 ， 小型
語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記
憶體需求持續上揚。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅增加產能，預估
2027 年新增產能有限，多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新
增產能實際貢獻要到 2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科新廠預計 2028 年放
量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，全產能將達 5 萬片/月。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 670 元 
南亞科為全球前五大 DRAM 廠，受惠於 AI 帶動之供給端改善，記
憶體產品漲價快速，南亞科今明年獲利創高，2028 年全球雖然有
新產能開出，但隨著 AI 由模型訓練與推理延伸至更多客製化邊緣
AI 應用，帶動記憶體位元需求長期成長。考量到 AI 應用滲透率提
升帶動記憶體需求，記憶體已成戰略物資，客戶亦開始簽訂長約，
參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 670 元，建議買進

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
南亞科成立於 1 995 年，為 DRAM （動態隨機存取記憶體）之研
發、設計、製造與銷售商，為全球第四大 DRAM 廠，市佔率約 
3%，由美光授權製程技術，其十二吋晶圓廠位於泰山南林園區，
月產能 6.8 萬片 ， 其中 20nm 製程約 3.8 萬片 ， 30nm 製程約 3 萬
片，公司產品廣泛應用於手機、PC、消費性電子產品，主要競爭
對手包括 SK Hynix、三星電子、美光、華邦電等。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. AI 帶動記憶體價格雙位數上漲 
全球 CSP 大廠持續加強投資 AI server 及相關基礎建設，全球九大
CSP 廠之資本支出持續高速成長，2026 年資本支出將達 9220 億元
(YoY+98%)，2027 年資本支出成長至 13830 億美元(YoY+50%)。
TrendForce 預估 2026 年 HBM 需求量年增 70%。而隨著 AI 應用由
LLM 模型訓練延伸至推論，CSP 廠擴大通用型伺服器布建，也使
記憶體拉貨重心由 HBM 延伸至 RDIMM ， 進一步推升 DDR5 價格。
3Q26 在 AI 與資料中心建置需求持續下記憶體仍嚴重供不應求 ， 儘
管價格基期已高，但預估 DRAM 合約價持續上漲，TrendForce 預
估第三季整體 conventional DRAM 合約價上漲 13-18% ， 第四季持續
上漲 10%~15%。供給方面，DRAM 三大原廠今年新增產能有限，
三星及海力士均以既有廠區調整及製程轉換為主，預估晶圓產能
分別增加 5%及 8%，而美光產能持平，三大原廠新廠產能開出並

明顯貢獻預估在 2028 年 。 預估 2026 年 DRAM 價格逐季調升 ， 2027
年新增供給有限下，價格易漲難跌。

2. 2027 年記憶體需求維持強勁 
展望 2026 第四季至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大，接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 (如: 
Edge、客製化)，小型語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置 (PC、手機、汽
車、機器人等)，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記憶體需求持續
上揚。需求面依各應用別而言，Server 方面，AI 及高效能運算持
續驅動 HBM 與 DDR5、LPDDR5 需求，高階 e-SSD、BMC、NIC
對 DDR4、LPDDR4 及 DDR5 需求顯著; PC 及手機方面，高階 PC
與手機對 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 需求維持穩定 ， 然中低階需求疲弱; 消
費型電子市場方面，AI 及一般伺服器對 HBM 、RDIMM 需求持續
擴張，排擠一般消費型記憶體供給。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅
增加產能，聚焦於高階雲端與高附加價值產品需求，DRAM 大廠
優先分配產能於 DDR5 及 HBM 產品 ， 預估 2027 年新增產能有限，
多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新增產能實際貢獻要到
2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科第二代 10 奈米級製程(1B) 16Gb DDR5 產
品交運量已達 10%，第三代 10 奈米級(1C)及第四代 10 奈米級(1D)
製程技術試產中。公司 DDR5 營收約占 10%，並可視需求彈性調
整增加，同時持續供應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 產品以緩解市場缺口。
公司亦積極與策略夥伴合作拓展 AI 邊緣運算驅動的記憶體市場，
目前處工程驗證階段，預計於 2026 年底推出新產品。南亞科 2025
年資本支出 134 億元，2026 年資本支出預計不超過 697 億元，其
中生產設備支出約占五成，新廠預計於 2027 年第一季開始裝機，
2027 年第四季開始生產，2028 年放量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，
預估 2030 年全產能將達 5 萬片/月。南亞科 2025 年位元銷售量成
長 50 多%，預估 2026 年成長 17~19%，預估 2027 年成長中低個位
數。

YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

### Page 1

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 IC製造 ‧ 台灣

台積電 
(2330.TW /2330 TT)

多、更多、再更多的 N3 產能擴張 
重要訊息 
台積電 3Q26 財報表現符合預期，管理層預估 2026 年營收將年增 44%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 符合預期，4Q26 展望強勁。3Q26 毛利率 66.4%，受 N2 量產爬坡稀
釋影響，較我們預估低 1.6 個百分點，但符合公司財測。受惠營收成長及營
業費用控管，營業利益及營益率均符合預期。台積電 4Q26 營收財測為 523
億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增 14%，較我們先前預估高出 7%，毛
利率及營益率則預期持平，主要受惠 HPC 需求強勁及 N2 量產爬坡。 
N3 持續擴產，並評估美國亞利桑那州以外的擴產機會 。隨 4Q26 展望轉
強，台積電將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先 40%以上上調至 44%，並
維持全年資本支出 600 億至 640 億美元。台積電過去鮮少將單一製程產能
擴充至超過原先規劃的目標( 約每月 15 萬片)。然而，除近期宣布的三座晶圓
廠(台南 Fab 18 、亞利桑那州二廠及熊本二廠) 外，台積電再宣布於台南新增
一座 N3 晶圓廠，預計於 1H28 量產。整體而言，我們預估 N3 產能將由
2026 年底的每月 15 萬至 16 萬片，在未來兩年進一步增加 8 萬片，增幅達
50%。我們認為此次擴產反映三項需求：(1)Agentic AI 帶動的 CPU 需求；
(2)HBM4e 客製化基底晶粒；以及(3)AI ASIC 。此外，台積電亦正評估在美國
亞利桑那州以外地區擴產的可能性，例如德州，但目前尚未做出最終決定。  
不會將任何商機拱手讓給競爭對手 。管理層多次強調不會讓競爭對手取得任
何商機，體現在 N3 產能的積極擴充。此外，儘管我們預期 Google(美 )委託
Intel(美 )生產的 Humufish 目前沒有備援計劃。然而，在必要情況下，台積電
將協助客戶確保產品如期交付， 暗示若 Intel 的 EMIB-T 製程進度落後，台積
電有望進一步提升市占率。部分投資人可能擔憂台積電導入 ASML High -NA
設備的時程較競爭對手晚兩年，進而影響其技術領先地位。但我們持不同看
法，採用最新設備並不必然代表技術領先。若 在 2030 年前台積電能在不投
資昂貴設備的情況下達到相近的製程效能，反而 有望取得相較同業更顯著的
成本優勢。 
投資建議  
儘管 3Q26 財報符合預期，且我們預估 4Q26 表現將更為強勁，但在市場預
期極高下，投資人仍可能對短期毛利率走勢有所疑慮。考量未來的成長動能
及穩健的執行能力，我們建議投資人趁股價回檔時逢低布局。我們僅微幅調
整獲利預估， 但隨評價基準遞延至 2028 年，我們 將目標價上調至 3,650
元，仍基於景氣上行週期平均的 20 倍本益比，維持「增加持股」評等 。 
投資風險  
全球景氣不確定性；積極擴產導致供給過剩。  
增加持股‧維持

台灣 台積電

法說會重點數據 
 4Q26 財測： 營收預估為 523 億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增
14%；毛利率 65-67%，營益率 56.5- 58.5%。 
 2026 年展望：全年營收成長預估上調至年增 44%，維持資本支出 600
億至 640 億美元。  
 毛利率稀釋：海外擴產預計稀釋 2026 年毛利率 2 個百分點，未來幾年
稀釋幅度將擴大至 3-4 個百分點。  
 產能規劃：  
 N2 製程家族產能預計於 2027 年增加至兩倍以上。  
 台南新增 N3 晶圓廠預計於 1H27 量產，另一座 N3 晶圓廠則規劃於
1H28 量產。 
 亞利桑那州 N3 二廠已完成廠房建設，目前正進行設備搬入及安
裝，預計於 3Q27 量產，略早於原訂時程。  
 日本 N3 製程預計於 2028 年量產。 
 A14 製程已開始進行產能準備，預計於 2027 年如期試產，並於
2028 年量產。

圖 5：公司概況  圖 6：台積電各製程晶圓營收比重  
台積電是全球最大的專業半 導體晶圓代工廠， 近年來其市占率
持續有所增加。 2013 年的八吋約當產能超越一千六百萬片。
台積電現有六座先進十二吋晶圓廠、六座八吋廠、一座六吋
廠，外加正在興建的一座十二吋廠。台積電提供以下服務：晶
圓製造，晶圓探測，組裝和測試，光罩生產和設計服務。

台灣 台積電

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 6

ESG 圖表定義  
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1天 = 8小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，凱基

## Document 118: 261026_ms_chroma

Source PDF: all report pdf/261026_ms_chroma.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

M
Idea
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
2H26 Driven by Semis and
Photonics; 2027 Seen as
Another Strong Year
A unique combination of power, electrical and optical testing
tool capabilities position the company well in AI deployment.
Stay OW.
We see 3Q26 EPS of NT$13.71: Chroma's 3Q26 revenue came in at NT$17,926mn,
up 32% QoQ and 180% YoY. We believe the strength was driven mainly by the
semiconductor and photonics businesses, while the power testing business likely

remained at an elevated level. Within the semiconductor and photonics businesses,
SL T  should benefit from AI HPC customers' ramping; meanwhile, optical testing
tools for transceivers and, to a lesser extent, CPO, are also seen as growth drivers
during the quarter. We estimate GM of 60.5% (flat QoQ) as scale tailwinds should
be largely offset by a less favorable product mix, while OPM should expand by
0.5ppt QoQ to 39.3% on some operating leverage. Overall, we expect EPS of NT

$13.71 (+13% QoQ/+15% YoY).
Growth momentum seen sustained into 2027: Looking ahead, we expect Chroma
to see another year of strong growth, supported by the power testing (51% growth
for 42% mix) and semi & photonics businesses (58% growth for 40% mix).
• Power testing: The ever-increasing power consumption of AI clusters should
continue to drive demand for higher spec testing tools. Along with our
forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit demand in the next

three years (see report), we think this should stay a major driver for Chroma.
• SLT: We are seeing more HPC chip designers adopting the system level
testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. As the first mover in this market. Chroma should benefit from
increasing chip volume as well as higher TDP, in our view.
• Photonics: Transceivers should continue to be the mainstream solution;
architectures such as CPO and NPO also need testing tools.

• Emerging business: We expect Chroma, by leveraging its existing customer
relationship and technology expertise, to gain more traction in FT handlers
and burn-in ovens for AI HPCs in 2027. These are the markets that Chroma
previously focused less on, so they represent new opportunities.
Stay OW: We remain constructive on Chroma for its testing equipment's unique
combination of power, electrical and optical capabilities, allowing it to capture
growth opportunities in various aspects of AI deployment. Our PT remains NT

$2,800 as our estimate increases are offset by a lower target multiple of 40x 2027e
P/E (previously 45x) to factor in generally more conservative sentiment toward the
AI space now. This implies a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

A Closer Look
Test instrument and ATS segment set to reach another
record high in 2026
Revenue for the segment increased 55% YoY to NT$10,546mn (37% of the mix) in 2025.
We estimate it will rise 118% YoY to NT$24,564mn (40% of the mix) in 2026. The ever-
increasing power consumption of AI clusters should continue to drive demand for higher
spec testing tools. Along with our forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit

demand in the coming three years (see our report), this should remain a major driver for
Chroma, in our view.

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 3: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 4: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 5: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

FT handlers to add another revenue stream
In HPC testing, Chroma used to focus mostly on the SL T side, although it did unveil its first
HPC FT handler earlier this year and is now going through the qualification process. We
believe this is a natural extension of its expertise and customer relationships accumulated
in SL Ts. We expect Chroma to gain more traction in FT handlers for AI HPCs in 2027.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Our price target (base case scenario value) remains  NT$2,800: Our PT is based on a
target multiple of 40x 2027e P/E (vs. 45x 2027e previously).   We lowered our multiple to
factor in recent volatility in market sentiment toward AI.   We view our multiple as justified
based on our estimated 68% operating profit CAGR over 2025-28. This implies a PEG of
0.6 vs. its average of 1.0 in the past decade, when the stock was trading at an average of

~19x forward P/E with an operating profit CAGR of 20%.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,900 (unchanged): We apply a 55x multiple (vs. 62x P/E
previously)  to our earnings estimate for 2027.  This scenario reflects stronger  5G and HPC
demand, driving more earnings upside.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,100 (unchanged): We apply a 16x P/E multiple (vs. 18x
P/E previously) to our earnings estimate for 2027. This scenario factors in weaker HPC
revenue as hyperscalers start to focus more on ROI for AI capex than they did in the past

couple of years.
Risks:
• Softer-than-expected AI capex
• Slower progress in advanced packaging capacity expansion
• Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

旺矽 (6223 TT)  Buy

旺矽營收優於預期 ，CPO 設備出貨將帶動評價提升 ， 給Buy
的投資建議。  
投資建議 ：旺矽營收優於預期， CPO 設備出貨將帶動評價提升，
給 Buy 的投資建議。  
09/2026 營收優於群益預期 ：旺矽 09/2026 營收為 20.48 億元，
MoM-0.02% 、 YoY+82.23% ，累計營收為 150.68 億元，
YoY+58.02% 。實際月營收與群益預估的 16.50 億元，差異為
19.43% ，優於預期。  
3Q26 優於市場預期 ：市場先前預期旺矽 3Q26 營收 QoQ+0~5% ，
實際營收 QoQ+12.76% ，優於預期。  
旺矽有佈局 CPO 測試設備 ：旺矽的 Insertion 3 設備預計 4Q26
向晶圓代工 、IC 設計 、OSAT 送樣 ，1H27 有望進入量產 ；Insertion 
2 設備預計 2H26 會有較明顯的結果。  
旺矽積極擴產： 旺矽表示，目前在許多客戶端都是第一供應商，
供貨比重大多為 80% ，但之前因為產能供不應求，有外溢到第
二供應商，現在有積極擴產。  
旺矽預計 2027 年提高 PCB 的自製率： 探針卡可分為測試頭、
載板及 PCB 。旺矽表示，探針及載板均已全數自製， PCB 預計
2027 年開始量產，目標將 PCB 的自製率提升至 50% 。  
群益預估旺矽 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 64.86 元 /110.49 元： 群
益預估旺矽 2026 年 /2027 年營收為 214.11 億元 /363.85 億元，
YoY+60.13%/+69.94% ；稅後純益 63.57 億元 /108.28 億元，
YoY+100.11%/+70.35% ；稅後 EPS 64.86 元 /110.49 元。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
旺矽考慮營運對生態效益之影響，促進及宣導永續消費之概念，並依下列原
則從事研發、採購、生產、作業及服務等營運活動，以降低公司營運對自然
環境及人類之衝擊。  
旺矽應評估公司經營對社區之影響，並適當聘用公司營運所在地之人力，以
增進社區認同。  
旺矽經由股權投資、商業活動、捐贈、企業志工服務或其他公益專業服務等，
將資源投入透過商業模式解決社會或環境問題之組織，或參與社區發展及
社區教育之公民組織、慈善公益團體及政府機構之相關活動，以促進社區發
展。  
旺矽遵循上市上櫃公司治理實務守則、上市上櫃公司誠信經營守則及上市
上櫃公司訂定道德行為準則參考範例，建置有效之治理架構及相關道德標
準，以健全公司治理。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收符合預期， 毛利率受 1H26 漲價提升 ， 預期 3Q26 毛利
將順利反應成本上漲與優化之產品組合 ， 毛利率預估提升 3%+ 。  
 AI 伺服器帶動高階銅箔基板需求強勁，同時驅動高階網路交換
器升級動能，銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸
續採用 CCL M8、M9 規格。 
 2H26 低軌 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火箭搭載低軌衛星，所採用之銅
箔基板升級至 M8 等級；以及，1.6T 光模組需求量增帶動銅箔
基板需求量增。

一. 2Q26 營收與財報符合市場預期，3Q26 營收優於預期  
台燿 2Q26 營收為 143.01 億元，+42.2%QoQ、+110.93%YoY，毛利
率為 29.72% ，稅後淨利為 23.42 億元，單季稅後 EPS+7.82 元。2Q26
台燿受到 ASIC CCL 專案出貨帶動，產品組合優化，毛利率已相較
1Q26 季增 4.5% 。台燿已公告 3Q26 營收為 179.48 億元(優於市場預
期 3%)，+25.50%QoQ，毛利率可順利轉嫁且高階 CCL 出貨比重提
升，預估台燿 3Q26 毛利率為 33.21%，稅後淨利 36.72 億元，單季
稅後 EPS+12.60 元。

二. AI 伺服器帶動 CCL/PCB 產值提升，產能擴充 33% 
宏遠投顧觀察，AI 伺服器與先進封裝等需求日增，高階 CCL 市場
需求將在 2024 年~2026 年 CAGR 達 26% ， 而高階 CCL 供給端 CAGR
則是 7%，高階市場需求殷切。高階銅箔基板(包含 AI 伺服器和高
階網路交換器，自 2024 年始採用 M8 CCL 材料；2026 年 M8 材料
佔總體 CCL 市場 40%，M8 材料佔比顯著高於 2022 年、2023 年的
22%和 26%。台系銅箔基板供應鏈當中，M6~M8 等高階材料的供
應廠商為(2383)台光電為首、(6274)台燿次之。回顧 3 年來，台燿
產能擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開
出，台燿總產能為 260 萬張/月，+13%YoY。展望 2027 年，台燿泰
國廠、常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120
萬張/月，預估 2027 年底時，總產能達 380 萬張/月，+46%YoY；預
估 2028 年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平
均 3 年每年產能擴充約 33% 。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交
投資建議：買進

換器(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿

年起 ， 除一般型伺服器 Intel Diamond Rapids 平台 、 AMD Zen6 Venice

品規格顯著升級。

三.  台燿高階網路 Switch 板材、低軌衛星板材、1.6T 光
模塊板材，27 年規格朝向高階型號發展 
展望 2H26，在高階 Switch 升級方面，800G Switch 採用 M8、M9 
CCL 規格，2H26 台燿將新增高階 Switch 客戶，隨著 AI 伺服器、
高階網路交換器產業發展朝向 800G、1.6T Switch 升級趨勢帶動之
下， 
銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸續採用 CCL M8、
M9 規格。此外，2H26 開始 800G 與 1.6T 光纖收發模組(採用 M8、
M9 CCL)開始放量，AI 資料中心帶動光纖收發模組需求，400G 以
上光纖收發模組出貨量 2026 年 1 億個、2027 年 1.45 億個、2028 年
達到 1.6~1.65 億個；在中系系統廠商積極拉貨之下，1.6T 光纖收
發模組 2026/2027/2028 年估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M ， 1.6T 光纖
收發模組，於 2 年內呈倍數增長。低軌大型 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火
箭搭載低軌衛星 ， 所採用之銅箔基板升級至 M8 等級 ， 材料損耗要
求降低，訊號傳出完整性標準提高，對於 CCL、PCB(HDI)、玻纖
與銅箔等規格預期同步升級，推升材料成本與 PCB 產值。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 38.49/66.22 元 
2026 年受惠 AI 伺服器及 ASIC 需求持續強勁的帶動下，台燿為網
通、高階網路交換器銅箔基板重要供貨廠商，將受惠 AI Server 對
於高階銅箔基板強勁的需求，因而，預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78
億元，+108.01%YoY，毛利率 31.13%，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，稅後
EPS+38.49元 。 隨泰國廠產能釋出 ， 預估台燿 2027年營收增至 963.61
億元，+55.73%YoY，毛利率為 33.26%，稅後淨利 198.23 億元，稅
後 EPS+66.22 元。

動，一般型伺服器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級
與相關供應的零組件商機，以及攻入 AI 伺服器、AI 高階網路交換

器的 CCL 、 低軌衛星 CCL 與新增的 1.6T 光纖收發模組規格轉換成

4

資料來源：GS 外資報告、宏遠投顧整理

二.2026~2027 年營運概況及展望 
1.HDI 與 M8 以上升級仍為關注焦點，2026~2028 年產能建置積極 
過往一般型伺服器用板採用 HDI 設計，強調細線路設計，CPU 尺
寸變大 Pin Count 數量增加至 2,000 只~4,000 只 Pin Count，PCB 板
面積不變之下要走向細線路化(線寬/線距)朝向更細線路設計。製
程上 HDI 經過多次壓合後，PP 在熱壓多次後，每一層熱膨脹係數
不同，導致通孔 Crack 或翹曲，製作時容易產生爆板，因此對於
PCB 板廠製作良率會是一大技術難關，而金像電、健鼎等廠商於
高階 HDI 相對具備生產優勢與競爭力，相對應台光電、台燿等高
階 CCL 供貨與升級也成為製程一大瓶頸；回顧 3 年來，台燿產能
擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開出，
台 燿 總 產 能 為 260 萬 張 /月，+13%YoY。展 望 2027 年，台 燿 泰 國 廠、
常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120 萬張/
月 ， 預估 2027 年底時 ， 總產能達 380 萬張/月 ， +46%YoY ； 預估 2028
年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平均 3 年
每年產能擴充約 33%。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交換器
(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿市佔
率得以提升。2027 年隨著 Nvidia Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+、M9
材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，台
光電與台燿等高階 CCL 新產能釋出，產品組合持續優化。

針對 AI GPU 與 ASIC 發展進程，Google TPU 出貨量從 2026 年 400
萬顆上修至 2027 年(V8 增量)增至 800 萬顆，倍數增長。AWS ASIC 
預計 2027 年出貨量達 330~350 萬顆，增長+21%YoY 。NVIDIA 體系
GPU，Rubin 系列層數提升至 24~26L，材料端從 M8/M8+往更高規
格升級，更高層數與多層式架構，促使 PCB 板訊號走線、供電完
整性、高速訊號之阻抗匹配控制、訊號傳輸完整性，皆帶動 PCB

6426 統新 
(Apogee Optocom Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/19 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率
躍升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。
2Q26 受惠生產效率提升、產能滿載毛利率躍升。 
 AI 伺服器帶動統新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要
骨幹濾片，光纖波段分離與光纖傳輸頻寬增加，會需要統新薄
膜濾光片作為訊號傳輸強化。除 Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分
散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，
2026~2031 年每年維持 31%年複合成長。 
 1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD，會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光
片。

一. 統新 2Q26 營收與財報優於市場預期，3Q26 續強 
統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率躍
升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。主要受
惠於 AI 資料中心互連需求，使得統新產線維持滿載。展望 3Q26
預估統新營收為 2.42 億元，+16.35%QoQ，毛利率 56.58%，稅後淨
利 1.06 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.76 元。統新預計產能增加 30%，以
因應客戶需求。

二. 2026 年 DWDM 隨 AI 骨幹網絡建置，濾片量增價漲 
統新高密度波長多工器( D W D M，D e n s e  W a v e l e n g t h  D i v i s i o n  
Multiplexing)，至少可傳輸 16 種不同顏色的光纖，最高可傳輸 128
種不同顏色之光纖，同一條主幹網路上，可以傳輸的頻寬因此加
大，相當具有傳輸效率。DWDM 於發送端將不同波長的光信號組
合起來(覆用, MUX)，並耦合到光纜線路上的同一根光纖中，進行
傳輸，在接收端又將多個波長的光信號分開(解覆用, DEMUX)，並
作進一步處理，恢復出原波長信號後送入不同的終端接收器。統
新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要骨幹濾片，波段分
離 ( S -B a n d、C - B a n d 傳 輸 核 心、L -B a nd 容 量 擴 充、S -B a nd 未 來 預 留、
U-Band 8nm 用於 OTDR 光纖監控偵測主幹網路是否具有斷點或需
要救援)，統新以單通道 100GHz 去細分 DWDM 濾光片，統新推出
新品，50GHz 間隔濾光片，支援的通道數量提升至 96Channels。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：375.00 元

三.  AI 伺服器高速傳輸，帶動光通訊主被動元件量增 
Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光
通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，2026~2031 年，DWDM 市場每年維持 31%
年 複合 成長。此 外，受惠 AI 伺服 器傳 輸速 率提升，如：Broadcom、
Marvell 等國際晶片廠商，於 2026 年起已推出 800G /1.6T Switch 晶
片組，等同於 AI 伺服器後端 Ports 支援的光纖通訊傳輸會一併升
級至 800G 與 1.6T 傳輸速率。Yole 報告指出，1.6T 光纖收發模組
出貨量，2026 年/2027 年/2028 年預估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M，
1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年內呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD ， 會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光片。
在薄膜濾光片鍍膜層數 ， Scale-Across 與 NPO 所需薄膜濾光片層數
約 150L~200L，未來發展至 CPO 時鍍膜層數會再增加 8L，統新為
此預計多準備 10 條產線預計開發 250L+規格，予台系知名晶圓代
工廠商。統新指出，Scale-Out 濾片鍍膜代工量增加，是替中系系
統廠代工(層數約 100~150L)。Scale-Across 濾片鍍膜層數會大於
200L，甚至高達 250L，目前訂單能見度高，涵蓋 1.)同業產能難以
支應，Cohr 交給統新、2.)中系系統廠商加單、3.)台系網通廠商於
低軌衛星鍍膜(LEO 非光學鍍膜) 。統新毛利率之所以跳升，是先前
客戶給統新的試產量僅 20~30 片，2Q26 起量產量增至 K 級別，出
貨量增加、規模經濟效應之下，單月營收損平點(評估為 0.4~0.5
億元)，2Q26 起月營收顯著跳升至 0.6~0.7 億元。預期此營運動能
至少能見度到 4Q26，但由於 DWDM 交期延長至 6~8 個月，預估
2027 年產業供需偏向緊俏。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 375 元 
統新競爭優勢在於其生產高品質高效率之薄膜濾光片篩選特定輻
射波段的光學元件，能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動
截面，藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中 
傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高光纖的使用效率，生產良率逐步提
升且具佔有一定之市場地位。預估統新 2026 年營收 8.56 億元，
+75.77%YoY，毛利率 53.43%，稅後淨利 3.32 億元，稅後 EPS 為
+8.63 元。展望 2027 年，隨產能積極擴充之下，預估統新年營收
為 17.02 億元，+98.83%YoY ，毛利率預估為 54.85% ，稅後淨利 5.57
億元，稅後 EPS+14.47 元。考量基礎建設帶動 DWDM 規格升級，
AI 伺服器傳輸速率提升因應 1.6T 傳輸薄膜濾光片用量同步增長，
給予統新投資建議買進，目標價 375 元(26.00 X2027 EPS)。

一.公司簡介

統新(6426)為生產精密光學元件，以及高密度分波多工器(DWDM)
關鍵元件─薄膜濾光片(TFF)之專業廠商。各產品線營收占比為主
動光學元件佔比低於 15%、被動光學元件：80~85%、光學鍍膜元
件與代工低於 10%。主要產品 DWDM 濾光片 (Dense Wavelength 
Division Multiplexer，即為高密度波長多工分工器)技術可以大幅提
高光纖通訊系統之頻寬，成為光纖通訊系統的市場中關注的焦
點。統新國內主要競爭對手為東典光電科技(股)，國外廠商包含
II-IV Cohernet(美國)、Iridian Technologies(加拿大)等廠商投入相關
產品研發及生產。

圖一、光通訊元件

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二.26 年營運概況及 26 年展望 
1.統新 DWDM 技術層層堆疊良率優異  
統新主要產品為薄膜濾光片即為篩選特定輻射波段的光學元件，
能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動截面，藉由不同光線
原理而選擇通過的光源(紅、藍、綠光) ，且藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技
術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高
光纖的使用效率。

2.因應 AI 資料中心基建商機，薄膜濾光片產品走向更高規  
回顧 5G 時期的薄膜濾光片，支援頻段增加，切割更細，中心波

### Page 5

5
Gogolook三大事業體
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整
⚫ Gogolook 是全球領先的信任科技 (TrustTech) 公司，運用自有防詐資料庫與 AI 技
術，提供消費者防詐、企業端防詐與金融科技服務。Gogolook 擁有全球最齊全的
通訊與數位防詐數據資料庫，涵蓋電話號碼、網址、和虛擬貨幣錢包地址等。

### Page 6

6
whoscall
⚫ whoscall 為 Gogolook 所
開發出的個人數位防詐
APP，全球下載量已突破
1億次，月活躍用戶超過
1,800萬，主要服務市場
涵蓋台灣、泰國、日本、
韓國、香港、巴西、馬
來西亞、菲律賓等。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 7

7
whoscall海外市場持續擴張
⚫ whoscall海外市場擴張成效卓越，海外營收占比於2025年已突破63%。泰國為
Gogolook最大的海外市場，其用戶規模、營收貢獻已超越台灣，但仍具備很大的
成長空間；公司於曼谷擁有龐大用戶基礎，目前正進軍泰國二、三線城市。菲律
賓與馬來西亞等地已具備百萬等級的活躍用戶，後續成長可期。
⚫ Gogolook於09/2026已正式和泰國政府退休基金(Government Pension Fund，GPF)簽
署MOU，並與國家跨行交易管理與交換公司(National ITMX)及泰國存款保障機構
(Deposit Protection Agency，DPA)合作，將whoscall防詐服務導入GPF體系，涵蓋
退休人員及現職公務員(超過120萬名會員)。此合作有助於同步推廣泰國當地B2C、
B2B業務。
⚫ 為了有效控制獲客成本，Gogolook在海外減少依賴成效較低的數位廣告，轉而與
當地政府、銀行及電信商合作。此外，考量到東南亞信用卡普及率較低，

Gogolook在泰國率先推出的「電信帳單扣款」訂閱模式大獲成功，目前此模式也
與台灣遠傳電信合作，藉此攻佔中高年齡層客群。

### Page 8

8
whoscall海外市場持續擴張
⚫ 針對品牌偏好較強或市場規模較小的國家(如日本與新加坡)，則透過白牌授權策略，
協助當地業者開發自有防詐App並從中分潤。
⚫ whoscall於02/2026~05/2026與日本Rakuten Mobile進行合作，推出「騷擾電話・簡
訊防護」活動，提供申請用戶免費試用，目前開始有試用用戶轉為付費訂閱，有
望挹注2H26日本市場消費者防詐業務成長動能。
資料來源：群益投顧彙整

### Page 10

10
Gogolook收購ScamAdviser
⚫ 企業端防詐方面，由於各國法規有加重企業防詐責任的趨勢，Gogolook將此業務
視為長期成長動能潛力來源。Gogolook於2024年收購具備超過6,000萬筆網域資料
庫的ScamAdviser後，成功拓展歐美市場。儘管2025年因主動調整部分低毛利率業
務，導致ScamAdviser營收下滑，但其實質獲利反而增加。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整
⚫ ScamAdviser成立於荷蘭阿姆斯
特丹，為全球防詐聯盟GASA
基石成員。ScamAdviser以AI演
算法與資料庫為基礎，開發自
有的網站風險評級技術，與
Gogolook的企業防詐解決方案
與電話號碼資料庫整合，為全
球最完整的通訊與數位防詐數
據資料庫。

### Page 11

11
Gogolook企業端防詐服務
⚫ 企業端服務主要分為「資料庫授權」與「商譽保護」兩大主軸：(1) 防詐情資方案，
為一種資料庫授權模式，將whoscall的「電話/簡訊」資料庫以及ScamAdviser的
「網域」資料庫，透過API、SDK方式授權給企業。(2) 商譽保護服務，企業的外
部防詐監控中心，協助企業或政府主動在網路上巡邏，偵測並下架冒用其品牌的
假投資廣告、假粉專或釣魚網站。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 12

12
Agent looker
⚫ 因應Agentic AI時代來臨，Gogolook於09/2026推出新產品Agent looker，工作原理
是在AI agent實際造訪或讀取網頁前，即時進行風險檢測並攔截惡意請求，可有效
避免AI agent被詐騙的風險。
⚫ 核心防護機制與功能：(1)毫秒級即時掃描，當AI Agent讀取外部網頁或執行URL
請求時，Agent Looker會在毫秒內對網頁封包與內容進行深度檢查，攔截惡意腳本、
釣魚網站以及針對 AI 模型的提示詞注入(Prompt Injection)攻擊。(2)Gogolook威脅
情資資料庫驅動。系統底層串接 Gogolook 累積多年的全球防詐騙、釣魚網站與風
險網域黑名單。基於海量真實風險樣本的數據庫，使其能快速且精準地辨識異常
行為。(3)活動稽核日誌。提供視覺化的管理儀表板，完整記錄AI Agent的所有外
部網路互動軌跡，包含造訪過的網址、讀取的網頁片段及最終的安全判定結果，
確保AI運作透明且可追溯。
⚫ Agent looker目前處於封閉測試(Private Beta)階段。

資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 13

13
金融科技-袋鼠金融
⚫ 金融科技為Gogolook於疫情期間因應線上詐騙激增而開創的新業務，包含全台灣
流量最大的金融媒合平台「袋鼠金融Roo.Cash」，以及專攻小額借款的「JUJI招財
麻吉」。袋鼠金融 Roo.Cash與國內金融機構合作，提供200件以上的金融商品方案
智慧比較與媒合。智能問答服務「Roo AI」，可協助用戶在信用卡、貸款、保險、
投資理財與民生優惠資訊中，快速找到最適合的選項。若順利成交，Gogolook即
可藉此獲得媒合收入。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 14

14
金融科技-JUJI招財麻吉
⚫ JUJI招財麻吉鎖定10萬元以下、傳統銀行不願承作的利基市場，自行舉辦機車貸
款、手機貸款等業務。該業務最核心的優勢在於將whoscall的通話行為數據轉化為
AI風控模型，不僅可嚴格控制核貸率，更將90天延滯率壓在極低水準。目前JUJI
招財麻吉應收分期款餘額持續增加，公司同時也把純風控模型顧問服務輸出至海
外市場，進一步擴大變現能力，2Q26已成功在泰國市場簽訂首批企業訂單。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

## Document 191: 7美元控住50.1%投票权：Anthropic的真实面目

Source PDF: all report pdf/7美元控住50.1%投票权：Anthropic的真实面目.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 招股书揭⽰其真实结构：七位创始⼈仅出资 7 美元认购 1 股 F 类股，即锁定公司
50.1% 投票权，亚⻢逊、⾕歌等战略股东均⽆投票权。同时公司以 8000 亿美元 “ 照付不议 ”
⻓协锁定算⼒，收⼊端 80% 按⽤量结算，财务结构更像重资产基础设施商，⽽⾮传统软件
公司。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 26 ⽇， Anthropic 正式向美国证券交易委员会公开提交 IPO 申请，股票代码 ANT ，⽬标估值
1.5 万亿美元⾄ 2 万亿美元。科技媒体已经把 “162 亿美元半年营收 ” 、 “807 亿美元净亏损 ” 、 “1.5 万
亿⾄ 2 万亿美元估值，有望超越 SpaceX 创下的历史记录 ” 翻来覆去写了个遍。我们再复述⼀遍数
字，读者⼤概只会打个哈⽋划⾛。
但这本⻓达 300 多⻚的招股书，值得慢读。因为它回答的不是 “Anthropic 多赚钱 ” ，⽽是⼀个更根
本的问题：当 AI 实验室⻓到万亿美元体量，它到底会变成⼀种什么样的公司？
翻完之后的感受是：它更像三家公司的合体 —— ⼀家算⼒ “ 能源 ” 采购商、⼀家 To B 企业软件公

司、⼀个带特殊治理结构的使命实验体。
下⾯五个反常识细节，拼出它的真实⾯⽬。
把算⼒当能源买：照付不议，最⻓到 2045 年
这是全⽂最值得慢读的⼀点。
Anthropic 在招股书⾥把算⼒称为 “ 关键投⼊ ” ，已签合同总额超过 8000 亿美元，最⻓延伸到 2045
年。券商按合同表汇总的⼝径是⾄少 6570 亿美元，差异来⾃统计范围 —— ⾕歌 2028 到 2031 年追
加的 1250 亿⾄ 1500 亿美元 TPU 采购，甚⾄没算进合同承诺表。
⼏笔⼤的包括：博通 AI 加速器设备租赁约 1612 亿美元，五年期不可撤销；⾕歌⾄少 1145 亿美
元，到 2033 年 7 ⽉；亚⻢逊⾄少 1100 亿美元，到 2036 年 4 ⽉；微软⾄少 449 亿美元，到 2036 年 4
⽉； SpaceX 的 AI ⼦公司，最⾼ 1205 亿美元，到 2029 年，预计 12 ⽉算⼒全部上线满载后约 31 亿
美元 / ⽉； AMD ，明年开始采购 Helios ，预计超 200 亿美元，另有期权可翻倍； Nscale 约 446 亿美

元， Lambda 约 413 亿美元。
重点不是总额⼤，是条款。⾕歌、亚⻢逊、微软的合同⾥都有同⼀个短语： “regardless of
actual usage”—— 不管实际⽤不⽤，钱都得付。⾕歌和亚⻢逊还约定，⽤量不⾜按差额补⾜。
这种 “ 照付不议 ” 条款，以前最常⻅的地⽅是天然⽓、电⼒的⻓期合同。
⽽ Anthropic 的收⼊端呢？约 80% 按实际⽤量结算，客⼾调⽤多少付多少。成本提前⼀年以上锁
定，收⼊随调⽤量波动。这是⼀套典型的重资产基础设施财务结构：经营杠杆极⼤，但⼀旦需
求不及预期，⻓期合同就是硬约束。
所以  Anthropic 本质上是在把算⼒当成能源来采购：⻓期合同、照付不议、成本提前锁定。⼤模
型公司的财务结构，正在越来越像⼀家重资产的基础设施公司。
Claude 封号这么狠，招股书⾥有⼀本账
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

在中国开发者眼⾥， Claude ⼤概是封号最严、⻛控最凶的 AI 产品之⼀。翻完招股书会发现，这
不是产品傲慢，是账本决定的。
2026 年上半年， Anthropic 营收 162.86 亿美元。其中按⽤量计费的收⼊约 131 亿美元，占 80% ；
订阅收⼊约 32 亿美元，只占 20% 。⽽这 20% 的订阅⾥，还包含企业 Team 和 Enterprise 套餐 ——
个⼈ Pro 和 Max 的占⽐，只会更低。
图⽚来源： Anthropic S-1 招股书
那⼋成的钱从哪来？企业和云服务商渠道。 48% 的收⼊通过 AWS 、⾕歌云、微软 Azure 的市场卖
出去；年消费 10 万美元以上的客⼾，从 2025 年 9 ⽉的 950 家，到 2025 年 12 ⽉的 1500 家，再暴增
到 2026 年 9 ⽉的超过 11900 家。
图⽚来源： Anthropic S-1 招股书
最能说明态度的是净收⼊留存率：约 900% ，意思是即⼀年前花 1 美元的客⼾，今年同期平均花 9

美元。但计算这个指标时，个⼈ Pro 和 Max 订阅⽤⼾被剔除在外，理由是 “ 固定价格订阅不能反映
扩张⾏为 ” 。
更直⽩的是免费⽤⼾。招股书把免费访问消耗的算⼒记在 “ 销售与营销 ” 费⽤⾥ ——2026 年上半年
这项费⽤ 31.68 亿美元，包含渠道、营销、⼈员和免费算⼒等。翻译⼀下：免费⽤⼾在账上是获
客成本的⼀部分。
Anthropic 给个⼈付费⽤⼾起了个词叫 “prosumers” ，研究⼈员、开发者、分析师、写作者。他们
的战略价值是当 “ ⽕种 ” ：⾃⼰先⽤上 Claude ，再回公司推动采购 Enterprise 。但算⼒永远不够⽤

### Page 3

的前提下，⼀边是⽉费固定的个⼈订⼾，⼀边是⽉花费⾼出 20 倍的 Claude Code 企业⽤⼾，资
源先给谁，账不难算。
⾄于中国⼤陆⽤⼾，招股书⾥只有⼀句制度性交代：公司有 “ ⽀持地区政策 ” ，实施基于 IP 的地理
封锁。本来就不在服务范围内。
7 美元，拿⾛ 50.1% 投票权
这⼤概是整份招股书⾥最反直觉的设计。
亚⻢逊、⾕歌这样的战略股东砸下重⾦， SBF 当年花 5 亿美元买 Anthropic 的股票 —— 他们拿到的
都是 N 类股，没有投票权。那这家公司的票在谁⼿⾥？
答案在附件⼀份有限责任公司协议第 10 条：名单上七个⼈，每⼈向公司出资 1 美元，合计 7 美
元，认购 1 股 F 类股。这 1 股没有经济权益，分不了⼀分利润，但投票权固定等于公司全部投票权
的 50.1% ，以后增发新股也稀释不掉。
七个⼈是 Dario Amodei 、 Daniela Amodei ，加上 Tom Brown 、 Jack Clark 、 Jared Kaplan 、
Sam McCandlish 、 Chris Olah—— 七位联合创始⼈。招股书对他们的定位叫 “ 我们使命的守护

者 ” 。
再往上还有⼀层：⻓期利益信托（ LTBT ）通常有权选举和罢免七名董事中的四名， Dario 和
Daniela 不能被信托撤换。公司同时属于纳斯达克规则下的 “ 受控公司 ” ，可以豁免部分治理要
求。
翻译⼀下：你可以买 Anthropic 的股票，但很难买⾛ Anthropic 的⽅向盘。资本市场承担股价波
动，创始团队掌握战略⽅向。这是为 “AI 安全使命 ” 设计的治理结构，也是普通股东要接受的前
提。
亏 807 亿也不慌， 8000+ 亿才是真账单
上半年净亏 807.48 亿美元，数字吓⼈。拆开看，其中约 723.88 亿美元来⾃可转换票据、衍⽣负
债和 SAFE 的公允价值变动。
机制不复杂：亚⻢逊投的钱有⼀部分是可转换票据，相当于⼀张 “ 以后按约定价格换股票 ” 的借
条。 Anthropic 估值今年 2 ⽉ 3800 亿美元， 5 ⽉升到 9650 亿美元，借条跟着⼤幅增值，会计准则要
求把增值部分记成公司亏损。招股书注明，这类变动主要由估值上升推动，不涉及现⾦流出。

按⾮ GAAP ⼝径，上半年调整后净利润是正的 26.71 亿美元；⼆季度经营利润 9.74 亿美元，经营
利润率 8% ，调整后经营利润率 19% ；上半年经营活动现⾦流净流⼊ 39 亿美元。
但 “ 纸⾯亏损 ” 不是核⼼⻛险。真正的账单是那 8000+ 亿美元算⼒承诺和随之⽽来的折旧。招股书
⾃⼰也警告，业绩可能因算⼒⽀出出现 “ 显著的期间波动 ” 。
积极的⼀⾯是效率在改善：每 1 美元算⼒投⼊带来的收⼊，从 2025 年⼀季度的 0.40 美元升到
2026 年⼆季度的 2.08 美元。原因包括训练效率提升、推理效率提升、同时在亚⻢逊 Trainium 、
⾕歌 TPU 、英伟达 GPU 和 AMD GPU 四类芯⽚上调度，以及新模型降价。

### Page 4

招股书中的「每  1 美元算⼒带来的营收」图⽚来源： Anthropic S-1 招股书
翻 译 ⼀ 下 ： 亏 得 吓 ⼈ 但 不 流 ⾎ ； 可 真 正 要 盯 的 ， 不 是 利 润 表 上 的 807 亿 ， 是 合 同 表 ⾥ 的
8000+ 亿。
估值与⻛险，第⼀次写进同⼀份⽂件
招股书的⻛险因素写了 90 多⻚，约为 SpaceX 同类披露的三倍，和企业常⻅的套话式披露很不⼀
样。
⽂件警告，其技术可能对⼈类构成 “ 灾难性或⽣存级⻛险 ” ，可能 “ 为意图开发化学、⽣物或其他
危险武器的恶意⾏为者提供实质性帮助 ” 。描述的失控场景包括模型未来可能出现 “ 意外的⾃我保
护⾏为 ” ，⽐如抵制关机、隐瞒或操纵信息，也可能⽣成类似 “ 勒索 ” 的胁迫性输出。
⾄于保险能不能兜底，招股书的说法是：没有任何单⼀保险公司，甚⾄整个保险业合计，也可
能⽆⼒承保。
还有⼏个细节值得记⼀笔：截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇的前九个⽉，约 5%~7% 的收⼊来⾃后来被认定违反使
⽤政策或商业条款的客⼾，原因是发现、调查到封号存在时间差，封号前已提供的服务收⼊照

常确认 —— 粗算约 16 亿 ~23 亿美元。 8 、 9 ⽉加⼤了封禁⼒度，公司提⽰这可能拉低未来收⼊和增
速。
七位创始⼈在⽂件⾥劝潜在投资者 “ 不要纯粹从交易⻆度看待你的购买 ” ，承认公司决策 “ 可能与
投资者预期背道⽽驰 ” ，因为利润最⼤化要与 “ 公共利益 ” 权衡。
⼀家准备以万亿美元级估值上市的公司，同时公开提⽰⾃⼰的技术可能带来⽣存级⻛险。估值
定价和⻛险定价，第⼀次被放进同⼀份⽂件⾥。
写在最后
合上招股书， Anthropic 的真实⾯⽬已经很清楚：收⼊像企业软件，成本像能源⻓协，治理像基
⾦会，⻛险则具有系统性、外溢性、公共性。
它是在把 AI 变成⼀种需要⻓期合同、能源级账单和特殊治理结构来承载的基础设施。华尔街买的
也是 “AI 成为下⼀代数字经济基础设施 ” 这张期权。
⾄于这张期权值不值 1.5 万亿到 2 万亿美元，接下来要看 10 ⽉份的 ARR （ Run-Rate Revenue ）、
看算⼒合同怎么落地、看企业客⼾的 Token 调⽤量能不能继续撑住那条陡峭的曲线。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

## Document 240: Anthropic全面杀入AI制药！收购“一人药企”，联手6大巨头，最新研发进度曝光

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic全面杀入AI制药！收购“一人药企”，联手6大巨头，最新研发进度曝光.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 收购单⼈⽣物科技公司 Mithril Medicine ， 18 个⽉内将罕⻅肌⾁萎缩症候选疗法
推⾄⼩⿏实验并获初步积极结果；同步与 BMS 、 Generate Biomedicines 等六⼤机构深度
合作， Claude 在分⼦设计、临床规划等环节均取得可量化进展，最快 2028 年底或启动⼈体
临床试验。
摘要：
Anthropic 正将 AI 能⼒从对话⼯具延伸⾄药物研发的核⼼流程。
据 Anthropic 官⽹ 10 ⽉ 27 ⽇发布的最新进展，该公司⽣命科学团队已与六家机构建⽴深度研究合
作，并收购了⼀家由单⼈运营的⽣物技术公司 Mithril Medicine ，直接下场开展药物研发。
Anthropic 表⽰，旗下 AI 模型 Claude 已在分⼦设计、制造⽂件审查、监管申报及临床试验规划等
多个环节展现出可量化的效率提升，部分早期数据已对外披露。
该公司明确表⽰，计划 " ⼤幅扩⼤ " 上述⼯作规模，并将持续将 Claude 的参与延伸⾄⼈体临床试

验阶段。
收购 “ ⼀⼈药企 ” ，亲⾃下场做药
Anthropic 此次最引⼈关注的动作，是收购了 Mithril Medicine——⼀家由创始⼈ Cody
Tranbarger 独⽴运营的微型⽣物技术公司。
Mithril Medicine 专注于两种罕⻅⼉童肌⾁萎缩症的治疗研究。 Tranbarger 此前借助 Claude 协调
与 11 家外部实验室的合同、物流和实验安排，并将公司⼤量运营事务委托给 Claude 处理。
结果是：Mithril Medicine 在不到 18 个⽉内，将其中⼀种肌⾁萎缩症的候选疗法从概念推进⾄⼩
⿏实验阶段，并取得了初步积极结果。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

Tranbarger 现已加⼊ Anthropic ，继续推进上述研究，并计划启动多个新项⽬。 Anthropic 表⽰，
若候选药物持续表现良好，预计最早于2028 年底具备启动⼈体临床试验的条件。
六⼤合作伙伴，覆盖研发全链条
在⾃主研发之外， Anthropic 过去数⽉已与六家机构展开合作，覆盖药物研发从早期发现到临床
规划的多个环节。
Generate Biomedicines ： CAR-T 疗法改造
Generate Biomedicines 正尝试将 CAR-T 细胞疗法从⾎液肿瘤扩展⾄实体瘤。六名科学家使⽤
Claude Code 为疗法的关键遗传指令⽚段提出新设计⽅案。其中⼀个⽅案⽐标准版本缩短约
70% ，实验室测试显⽰，免疫细胞产⽣的受体数量约为原来的10 倍，肿瘤细胞杀伤效率更⾼，
免疫细胞活跃时间也更⻓。
Divergence Labs ：⻝物过敏新疗法
Divergence Labs 正在开发⼀种针对⻝物过敏的新型药物，⽬标是将 IgE 分⼦从免疫细胞上完全

剥离，⽽⾮仅中和⾎液中游离的 IgE 。 Claude 将其研发⽬标转化为详细设计⽅案，并在约⼀个⽉
内近乎⾃主地⽣成了约3.5 万个抗体设计⽅案。早期筛选中，研究⼈员找到了针对两个功
能 " 臂 " 的有效候选： 3 个能与 IgE 结合， 20 个能将 IgE 从免疫细胞上剥离，其中 3 个的效果优于现
有标准药物。
BigHat Biosciences ：分⼦优化
BigHat Biosciences 将 AI 与⾃动化实验室结合，⽤于优化候选药物分⼦。在约 10 周内，团队测试
了约1,010 个分⼦变体。中途， Claude 与研究⼈员重新分析原始数据，调整了评估指标和实验⽅
案。最终结果显⽰，最优版本的结合⼒提升逾10 倍，靶向癌细胞的候选分⼦热稳定性提⾼
18°C，另⼀候选分⼦的聚集速度降低逾100 倍。
Enveda ：⽂件审查与监管申报
Enveda 的制造化学家此前有多达 75% 的⼯作时间耗费在⽂件处理上。 Enveda 构建了⼀套将制造
⽂件接⼊ Claude 的系统，⽤于⾃动标记潜在问题。在对1,061 ⻚⽂件的测试中， Claude 识别出了

⼈⼯审查员发现的全部 11 个问题，并额外标记出 7 个被⼈⼯遗漏的问题。整体⼈⼯审查时间从逾
25 ⼩时压缩⾄不⾜ 3 ⼩时，降幅达 89%。
此外， Claude 还起草了两份重要监管⽂件。其中⼀份耗时4 ⼩时，⽽⼈⼯完成同类⼯作通常需要
约3 个⽉。

多、更复杂的药物项⽬。⽬前， Pleiades 已开发出⾸个 Claude 技能模块，⽤于评估药物靶点的

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Recent revisions to US PCE inflation, UK productivity
and Canadian population estimates are reminders that
statistical measurement is imperfect. Revisions are both a
fact of life and, when they improve measurement, desirable.
• Australian data will also be revised over time, though

not necessarily in the same ways as these latest revisions
abroad. The ABS tends to favour quality over speed,
which reduces the need for early revisions as more data
become available. But revisions can still matter. Population
estimates implied in the initial releases of labour market and
GDP data are especially likely to be revised in the near term.
• Estimates of current trends can change as future data are
published. Shifting seasonal patterns are another source

of revision, especially for monthly CPI components with
short histories. Series that are derived from multiple other
imperfectly measured series are more prone to revision
than quantities that can be measured directly. All these
considerations point to a need for humility when using
recent data to make strong policy pronouncements.
On September 30, the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation,
PCE, was revised down. New methods and data sources were
applied to three components of services inflation (financial

advice, software and legal services), large enough to result in
downward revisions to the overall read on core PCE inflation.
These changes had been pre-announced in June and were
therefore not a surprise. The CPI measure of inflation was not
affected, nor should the revisions materially change your view
of US inflationary pressures.
Some revisions are more consequential for interpretation and
policy. A couple of weeks before the US PCE revision, it was

revealed that the UK Office for National Statistics was revising
down its estimates of average hours worked per worker. The
result was that measured productivity growth was nowhere
near as weak as had been widely believed. As my friend and
counterpart, TS Lombard’s Chief Economist Freya Beamish
commented on social media, “Oh well. Not like any major policy
decisions were taken on the basis of those numbers.” Quite.
Also in September, Statistics Canada revised up its estimates

of Canada’s population, taking the recent growth rates
from outright contraction to still-slow growth a little below
½%yr. The number of temporary residents in Canada is still
falling, but the revised data do a better job of tracking them,
especially those seeking an extension on their permit. (An alert
When reality gets revised
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
for customers who have seen the cross-country population
graph I often use at client events: the Canadian line now looks

different but still shows a sharp slowing.)
There is no fault to be attributed here. Revisions to key
statistics are a part of economic life. Sometimes the underlying
data arrive with a lag, perhaps because of reporting deadlines.
Sometimes the compiling agency improves its methods or
gains access to a better data source. The US and UK examples
above fall into this category. In the Canadian case, better
data and a more accurate definition of scope were involved.

All these revisions should be welcomed. They provide a more
accurate picture of the underlying reality.
Australia, revised
Revisions are a fact of life for Australian statistics, too. I am
still a little scarred by that unexpected –0.5%qtr GDP print
when the September quarter 2016 data were first released on
my first day as RBA Assistant Governor; the figure has since
been revised to 0%qtr and was followed by a big bounce-back
in the December quarter.

The recent revisions mentioned above are less likely to be
replicated in Australia, though. Australia’s CPI is designed such
that the headline (not seasonally adjusted) measure is never
revised. This is because it is used in many legal contracts.
If an error is detected, the ABS makes the adjustment in
subsequent months and quarters rather than revising the
history. Our labour force survey has a much higher response
rate than its UK equivalent – Australians are a compliant lot

when the ABS comes knocking – and measures actual hours
worked more reliably. And our population statistics already
handle the array of temporary resident visas with the Bureau
instead applying a standard 12-month-in-16-month residency
test to count as a resident for the population statistics rather
than using a visa-classification basis.
This last case illustrates Australia’s broader tendency to
favour quality over speed in official statistics. Our CPI and

national accounts data tend to be published a bit later than
peer countries’ data for the same reference period, and our
“Economic data are often revised. Sometimes
the revisions change the story; sometimes
they do not."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
estimated resident population data is quite a bit lagged
relative to Canada’s (the 12-month-in-16 criteria taking over
a year to confirm). The trade-off is that observers of the
Australian economy must wait longer but can be less worried
about “flash” and “advance” releases of data that can be

quickly revised to tell a completely different story.
Revisions to data still can matter in Australia, and it helps
to be aware of the specific cases where it might occur. For
example, precisely because the population data are very
lagged, the population estimates underlying the initial
releases of the labour force survey and national accounts are
based on preliminary data and get revised later as a matter
of routine. This is why ratios such as the unemployment,

employment-to-population and participation rates provide a
better real-time gauge of labour market conditions than the
monthly change in employment. Similarly, GDP per capita can
be revised even when GDP is not. This type of revision is also
a more likely source of short-term changes in measured hours
worked in the Australian productivity data than the conceptual
change that shifted the UK data.
The usual reporting lags – and occasional correction of

reporting errors by the reporting entity – will always be a factor
in Australia just as it is in other countries. The annual national
accounts, due on 23 October, incorporate additional data, revise
the quarterly estimates and provide first estimates of important
quantities such as the capital stock. Just as importantly, though,
the annual accounts reconcile the different ways of measuring
GDP. In doing so, what we thought we knew about the recent

history of output growth can change.
Every purchase is also a sale, so conceptually, you should get the
same answer for final output regardless of whether you count
how much is produced, how much people spent on it, or how
much income people got for producing it. In practice, though,
the “production”, “expenditure” and “income” approaches do
not always line up until the ABS goes through a more thorough
reconciliation process in compiling the annual accounts.

When the future changes the past
Future data can also change our view of the past. Two good
examples are trend estimation and seasonal adjustment. Future
observations help reveal whether an apparent trend is stable.
As a result, real-time trend estimates can differ considerably
from those calculated with the benefit of later data.
Identifying stable seasonal patterns also requires a reasonable
run of data. Future observations affect whether an earlier

movement is judged to be normal seasonal variation. This
problem is more pronounced for newer series, where there is
not yet enough history to establish what “normal” looks like.
Consider, then, the monthly CPI. This relatively new measure has
only a short run of data with many individual components having
too little history to use standard seasonal adjustment methods.
As Westpac’s senior economist Justin Smirk has previously
highlighted, some components such as footwear are being

‘seasonally adjusted’ by applying Henderson trends, similar
to those used in the ‘trend’ measures of labour market and
national accounts data. For some other components, including
books, furniture, audio-visual and computing equipment and
children’s clothes, certain price falls – usually associated with
sales periods – are just arbitrarily removed from the seasonally
adjusted series. The index is held at its previous month’s level
but otherwise matches the non-seasonally adjusted series.

The seasonally adjusted measures of these CPI components will
inevitably be revised over time, and thus so could the monthly
trimmed mean estimates. This helps explain why the RBA has
said it would, for now, focus on the quarterly trimmed mean
based on the old seasonal factors. That measure will at least
not be buffeted by shifting measurement of seasonality in a
short history. If seasonal patterns are themselves also shifting,
the current underlying trend might look a bit different in a few

years, not just a smoother month-to-month profile.
The broader point here is to remember that economic statistics
are a lens on reality. Sometimes the lens is smudged, sometimes
the scope is off and sometimes what is being read as underlying
trends is wrong. Measures constructed from several imperfect
series – productivity from output and hours worked, or unit labour
costs from productivity and labour costs, or savings rates derived
from income and spending – are especially prone to revision

compared with concepts that can be measured more directly, like
a price. That is a strong argument for humility before making firm
policy pronouncements based on simple data comparisons.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
consistent with a labour market that is gradually normalising
than one entering a meaningful downturn.
The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish
message, with participants noting they had "not seen

sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and
expressing concern that inflation risks remained "skewed to the
upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing
persistent goods price strength to the effects of the AI buildout
despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was
also concern that, after more than five years of inflation above
target, elevated inflation could begin to influence inflation

expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In
contrast, labour market conditions were generally viewed as
stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority
noted the labour market had strengthened somewhat recently,
several participants highlighted unusually low dynamism,
characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding
rate and elevated long-term unemployment. Importantly,
participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth

was moderate and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2
percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor market
was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".
While participants broadly expected AI-related investment to
boost productivity growth and potential output over time, several
warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand
to outpace aggregate supply over the medium term, putting
upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some

participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate
and several judged the current policy stance to be "not restrictive
or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants
supported the September rate increase, and most assessed
that another increase in the federal funds rate would likely be
appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain
data dependent.
The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted

a similar picture of an economy that remains resilient but is
gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9
from 55.4 in August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in
business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export orders, which
slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight
The only data release of note in Australia this week was
the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Survey ,
which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At

80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to deeply pessimistic
levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock
when readings were among the weakest in the survey’s
more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest
rate pressures are once again clearly front of mind for
consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a
year ago’ and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0%
and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the RBA’s

September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact –
sentiment tanked 20% between those surveyed before the
RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.
Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient,
but they still weakened, with the one-year and five-year ahead
sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers
are becoming more ‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not
outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively

low versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high
of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip demand. We
expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force
Survey – see our preview for more detail. All told, with higher
interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening
jobs market all weighing at once, households are entering the
final months of the year on the back foot.
Offshore, the focus was on US data.
Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that

September non-farm payrolls came in softer than expected
across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k
expected), and there were sizeable downward revisions
to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July
and August payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k
respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure
& hospitality and education & health services industries,
although construction and manufacturing employment
continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related

investment. The unemployment rate edged up to 4.2%, with
softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force
participation to 61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated
to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average
pace of job creation slowing to around 50k. However, softer
payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market
deterioration. Lower immigration and ageing demographics

have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“A key focus was the interaction between
AI investment, productive capacity and
inflation."

months. Labour market conditions improved modestly with
the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory
50.1 after two months below 50, supported by elevated

illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Fuel, housing and the bumpy road ahead
Higher fuel costs, a weaker exchange rate, and a softer
housing market have prompted a refresh of our near-term
forecasts. Inflation looks set to finish 2026 above 4%, while
growth could lose a little momentum into year-end. Even so,

we continue to expect the RBNZ to pause in October before
lifting the OCR to 3% in December.
New Zealand’s recovery is still moving forward, but the road
has become rougher. Since our last forecast update in August,
global oil and refined-fuel prices have climbed sharply, the
New Zealand dollar has fallen, and longer-term interest rates
have moved higher. At home, the housing market has remained
weaker than we anticipated. None of those developments,

on their own, are enough to derail the recovery. Together,
however, they point to a tougher finish to 2026 for households
and businesses. We have therefore revisited our near-term
inflation, housing and growth forecasts. The broad story
remains one of continued economic expansion and gradually
improving labour demand, but with a more pronounced
inflation spike and slightly less momentum in consumer
spending. Our OCR call is unchanged: a pause at the October

review, followed by a 25 basis point increase to 3% at the
December Monetary Policy Statement.
A temporary inflation reprieve
We continue to think that consumer prices rose 0.7% in the
September quarter. That would lower annual inflation from
4.1% in June to 3.7% in September, broadly consistent with
the monthly price updates published so far. The final monthly
release for the quarter is due on 16 October and will help firm up
that estimate. But any easing in the annual rate is likely to prove

short-lived. We now expect prices to rise 1.1% in the December
quarter, lifting annual inflation to a three-year high of 4.2% by
year-end. That is half a percentage point above our previous
projection and above the RBNZ’s September forecast of 3.9%.
The reason is easy to see at the petrol pump. Higher global
fuel prices have been amplified by a more than 4% fall in
the New Zealand dollar since the start of September. Local
petrol prices have risen by around 50 cents a litre over the

past month, with the nationwide average price of 91 unleaded
reaching a record $3.55 a litre this week. RBNZ Governor
Breman acknowledged a couple of weeks ago that higher
oil prices have tilted the risks around the near-term inflation
outlook to the upside. Importantly, however, the RBNZ remains
focused on the medium-term inflation outlook rather than
trying to offset every movement in volatile fuel prices.
For now, we have left our quarterly inflation forecasts for 2027

unchanged. Even so, the higher starting point at the end of this
year means annual inflation is likely to remain firm through much
of 2027. Our updated profile has inflation at 3.7% in March
2027, 2.6% in June, 2.5% in September and 1.7% in December.
That is broadly similar to the RBNZ’s path through the first three
quarters, although our forecast falls more quickly late in the
year. We will take a fuller look at the 2027 inflation and growth
outlook next week alongside the monthly Market Outlook.

Housing fails to find its feet
The housing market has also delivered a disappointment. We
had expected prices to stabilise through the second half of
2026, but sales volumes have continued to weaken, and the
median time required to sell a property has lengthened to 48
days. Average prices have kept edging lower, despite firmer
conditions in parts of the South Island with greater exposure
to the strong export economy. The latest indicators do not
suggest that a decisive turn is close. We have therefore revised

our forecast for national house price growth in 2026 from a
0.6% rise to a 1.4% decline. That incorporates quarterly falls
of 0.5% in September and 0.8% in December.
The headwinds are familiar: the labour market remains soft,
mortgage rates are higher and employment growth is still
moderate. Those forces are likely to keep the market subdued
for some time, with risks tilted towards another weak year
in 2027. Changes to the taxation of capital gains on investor

housing following the 2026 General Election may add another
potential drag.
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Regional outcomes will continue to differ. Tourism- and
agriculture-focused areas should benefit from stronger income
and employment growth, supported by robust commodity export
prices. We have lifted our 2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price forecast to
$9.75/kg and set an opening forecast of $9.50/kg for 2027/28. In

contrast, homeowners in the major North Island cities may have to
wait longer for the wealth boost that comes with rising property
values. Data released this week shows that household wealth
continues to be dragged down by declining housing values.
A little less momentum
The weaker housing backdrop matters for the wider economy.
When petrol absorbs more of the household budget and home
values are drifting lower, consumers tend to become more
cautious. That combination is likely to crimp discretionary

spending in the run-up to Christmas and could take some
steam out of the construction recovery. That’s even though our
own card spending data for September  showed resilience in
consumer spending. Recent business and consumer confidence
surveys have nevertheless remained resilient despite the rise
in energy prices and long-term interest rates. Our short-term
growth forecasts therefore remain broadly intact, but we have
trimmed our December quarter consumer spending projection

and now expect GDP growth of 2.5% over 2026. That is still
a solid result, although the balance of risks has shifted to
the downside.
There are several moving parts to watch. Sustained high fuel
prices would squeeze household purchasing power more than
we have allowed for. The sharp lift in long-term interest rates
may also weigh on activity through 2027, even if shorter-
term borrowing costs evolve as expected. The election result
introduces two-sided risks that are difficult to quantify before

the shape and timing of policy changes are known. Against that,
strong agricultural incomes and signs of improving employment
growth should provide a buffer. We have not changed our 2026
labour market forecasts. Hiring appears to be strengthening
gradually, but it will take time before that improvement
translates into a noticeable fall in the unemployment rate.
The RBNZ should still pause before Christmas
Despite the higher near-term inflation track, we are not

changing our OCR forecast. Since the July Monetary Policy
Review, our view has been that the RBNZ would slow the pace
of increases as the OCR approached the Bank’s estimate of a
neutral 3%. The September meeting clearly signalled a pause
in October and one further rise in December. We continue to
regard that as the most likely path. The latest developments
raise headline inflation but, so far, there is little evidence that
inflation expectations are moving higher or that the fuel shock

is generating broader, persistent price pressure. At the same
time, growth risks warrant some caution.
A pause in October would give the Monetary Policy Committee
time to assess the cumulative effect of the increases already
delivered and to refine its strategy for 2027. Financial
conditions have also tightened from an unexpected source:
longer-term interest rates have risen markedly over the past
six weeks. That increase has at least offset the easing impulse

from the lower exchange rate, even though the weaker
currency is adding to imported inflation in the near term. We
still think the RBNZ is likely to have more work to do in 2027,
but there is plenty of data to come before that judgement
needs to be made.
For now, the best description of the outlook is resilient but
less comfortable. Export incomes remain strong, activity is
expanding and the labour market is beginning to improve. But
households face an awkward combination of record petrol

prices, soft home values and higher borrowing costs. That
mix points to a bumpy end to the year: inflation will be hotter,
spending growth a little cooler and the recovery more uneven
across regions. The RBNZ can afford to wait in October, but if
the economy develops broadly as we expect, a further hike to
3% in December will still be needed.

AUS: Sep Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Oct 15, Last: 39.5, Westpac f/c: 20 
Market f/c: 20, Range: flat to 35
Employment surprised to the upside in August with a 39k gain. 
Looking through the monthly swings, while momentum has 
improved over recent months, employment growth has largely 
continued to bump around below its historic average and the 
current pace of working age population growth.
Our preview provides a deeper look into the various gauges 
of labour demand – the LFS suggests employment growth 
is sluggish but broader measures in the Q2 Labour Account 
showed a stronger narrative. Meanwhile job ads and business 
surveys are also pulling in different directions.
For September, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of 
+20k, which would see annual growth hold in recent ranges.
AUS: Sep Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Oct 15, Last: 4.6, Westpac f/c: 4.6 
Market f/c: 4.6, Range: 4.5 to 4.7
The participation rate jumped to 67.1% in August, just under 
the 115-year high recorded in January 2025. With labour 
supply once again outstripping demand, the unemployment 
rate rose from 4.5% to 4.6% – the highest since in late-2021, 
close to rounding up to 4.7% (4.646% at three decimal places).
A softer economy and sub-par employment growth typically 
discourages some people to search for work, but cost-of-living 
pressures and interest rate rises are more than outweighing this 
effect, encouraging more people into the labour market.
With the participation rate expected to pare back to 67.0%, we 
think the unemployment rate will hold steady at 4.6%. Please 
see our preview for more detail.

### Page 9

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.9
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Sep REINZ House Sales and Prices (%ann)
Oct 14, Sales Last: –13.0, Prices Last: –0.9
The housing market has remained soft in recent months,
with sales falling to a two-year low and prices edging lower.
Mortgage rates have risen as the RBNZ has initiated a new

tightening cycle, and a growing supply of unsold homes on the
market means that buyers are under no pressure to bid prices
higher. Conditions remain stronger in the south, with prices
up on last year in Canterbury, Otago and Southland, while
Auckland and Wellington are the weakest markets.
NZ: Sep Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Oct 16, Last: –0.9, Westpac f/c: 0.8
We’re forecasting a 0.8% rise in retail spending in September,
reversing the 0.9% fall seen in the previous month. In part, the

forecast rise in spending is due to the sharp increases in petrol
prices in recent weeks. Those higher fuel costs are weighing on
spending in other categories like hospitality. Nevertheless, we
still expect a 0.5% rise in core (ex-fuel) spending. Stats NZ’s
monthly retail spending figures have been very choppy for the
past two years, and we’re likely to see a bounce following last
month’s unexpected falls in categories like furnishings.
NZ: Sep Selected Consumer Prices (%mth)

Oct 16
This will be the last monthly update ahead of the full quarterly
CPI release on 22 October. We’re currently forecasting a 0.7%
quarterly rise in the CPI (+3.7%yr) and will be watching the
detail of the Selected Prices update to pin down that forecast.
In terms of the big categories, we’re forecasting a roughly 6%
rise in petrol costs, along with a 0.7% rise in food prices. That’s
balanced against continued softness in housing rents (which
are the single largest component of the CPI). The key sources

of risk for our forecast are the volatile travel categories, most
of which recorded sizeable drops last month that we expect to
partially reverse in September.

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.
